## §Σ · FINDING → DW-LAW MAP, THE ENGINE-GAP FLAGS, THE OPEN-QUESTIONS LEDGER, METHOD, TALLY

### §Σ.1 · The nine laws, as this tranche found them biting

| DW law (DESIGN §2) | What the faith/learning evidence says | Families |
|---|---|---|
| 1 FUNCTIONS, NOT ROOMS | Liturgical and learning fixtures ARE the functions: focus, screen, font, chest, carrel, lectern, stand, squint — the floor church has one room and six functions; prosperity splits them (vestry, porch chamber, chapels) and the ceiling duplicates (nine altars, 19 chapels, 88 desks). | C, G, M, P |
| 2 THREE-CLAMP CEILING | Clamp (c) is VIOLATED by the faith register: Munich's hall for 20,000 in a town of 13,000; Lübeck's cathedral-scale parish church; the cathedral licensed by a SEE in a village of 1,751 — the faith building out-prospers its town by ENDOWMENT and PILGRIMAGE income; the clamp needs an `endowment` escape valve, owner-gated. | C, G |
| 3 THE FLOOR IS EXISTENCE | The floor is often NO BUILDING (shrine, walk, yard, grove) or a HOSTED fixture (chest, altar, school in the nave); decline sheds in the stated order the evidence gives: nave first / chancel first (C), lead roof → tile (F), chained books → candlestick-scourers (M), the loft and rood ordered down (C), the west gallery removed (H). | A, B, C, E, F, H, I, M |
| 4 DERIVE, DON'T STORE | No conflict; the RIGHTS vector (B) and the licence/endowment EVENTS (§Σ.2) are the circumstances the derivation must read. | B |
| 5 STABLE ANCHORS | The richest fossil set in the program: the socket stone without its shaft (A), the piscina in a cottage (B), the rood stair in a pier (C), the blocked charnel (E), the converted cloister as a house (F), the stable in the nave (G), the truncated library over the walk (M), the buried henge stones and the village inside the ring (I), the movable stave church (P). A RE-USE enum on the PRECINCT and a `supersededBy` between institutions across eras are the two carriers law 5 lacks. | all |
| 6 FRONTAGE FROM THE PARCEL | Faith buildings are oriented by AXIS (east; the solstice; the meridian; the orientation wall), not by the street — London's tower-porch entrance vs Norwich/York's nave door is a regional fashion; the chapel's door moves for a stream (Madron). Law 6 needs an `orientedAxis` override for this institution class. | A, C, G, I, N, P |
| 7 BASEMENTS = UNDERCITY | The crypt, the charnel, the buried grove, the cave-temple, the secular vault under a church (All Hallows Honey Lane) — four surface joints typed here (aisle stair, porch stair, external door, chancel passage) for the undercity's components. | C, E, I, P |
| 8 THE PARTI DRAW | The closed enum has NINE building members (BASILICAN, CLAUSTRAL, COLLEGIATE_COURT, MOSQUE_COURT, SYNAGOGUE_HALL, CENTRALIZED, STAVE, MEETING_HOUSE, TEMPLE_CELLA — eleven with NONE_HOSTED and NO_BUILDING); the draw is weighted by CULTURE × endowment × era, NEVER by deity rank; two members (CENTRALIZED-round, COLLEGIATE_COURT) are shared with R-INST-1 (keep) and DWR1A (the manor court). | all |
| 9 VERTICAL HONESTY | The faith register is the program's tallest: naves 8–48.5 m, towers 110–131 m, spires 277–404 ft (ranges for lost ones); the double-height hall is the rule not the exception (the college hall "like a solarium above ground"; the stave church's raised central roof; the library's 7 ft 6 in window sills); a cell whose roof MOVES (the observatory crypt). | C, G, L, N, P |

### §Σ.2 · The engine-gap flags (for DW-0 / the owner), in priority order

1. **`parti` on `INTERIOR_KINDS.faith` (and `learning`).** The engine's `faith` template (nave front + sanctuary back + vestry at tier ≥ 2) is the BASILICAN two-cell floor and nothing else; `learning` (reading + stacks + study) is the lectern/stall library and nothing else. Nine parti members are unrenderable today (§17.f). PRIORITY 1 — nothing in this tranche can be composed without it.
2. **The ENDOWMENT / LICENCE / SEE event stream (DESIGN §3's "tenure/endowment", which has NO field in the clean tree).** Found load-bearing at every upgrade from NO_BUILDING to OWN BUILDING: the chapel of ease's licence and RIGHTS vector (B), the rectory tenure that owns a tithe barn (D), the charnel's chaplains (E), the monastery's count 4 → 1,000 (F), the see that makes a village a cathedral city (G), the endowed schoolroom (K), every college and great library (L, M), the patron's observatory (N). The three banked findings at the checkpoint (§0) stand; this is the first of them, now with seven witnesses. PRIORITY 2 — a DW-1 vocabulary item: `endowment: {grade, donor, year}` + `licence: {authority, rights[], year}` on a faith/learning institution.
3. **`exclusiveGroup: 'religiousCenter'` at city tier** (the third banked finding): a cathedral city holds friaries, a nunnery and 20–108 parishes (Chester's abbey + nunnery + three friaries; Norwich 58; London 108). Lift `Multiple monasteries` out of the group or give the cathedral its own. At town the placement is correct. PRIORITY 3 — a one-line catalog change, owner-gated (catalog = persistence shape).
4. **The catalog learning gap** (the second banked finding): no school, university, library-below-metropolis, observatory, anchorhold or scriptorium at any tier. Three rows would close it (song/grammar school at village/town HOSTED; parish/chapter library at town/city HOSTED; university at city) — and NO scriptorium row (§16: it resolves into carrels and study closets). Owner-gated (new capability vs repair: these are new rows).
5. **Deity rank licenses nothing structural** (§1 C4): `major/minor/cult` → `religious_authority` is the engine's only faith input and no fetched source maps it to a building; the ONE plausible mapping is the shrine-station count at the pilgrimage grade (G). Flag: do not let the grammar scale a temple by rank; let it scale by endowment (item 2) and culture (item 1).
6. **The SCREEN primitive** — a wall-with-passage inside a through-room (1.6 m at Escomb; a cell with a stair at the cathedral grade). The §452 set has no partition-with-passage; propose `SCREEN{passage_w; loft; stair}` as a fixture-class joint (parish) and `SCREEN_CELL` (cathedral), with a VISIBILITY split that is not a REACHABILITY split.
7. **`sizeFrom: host`** — the library (over the walk), the charnel (under the aisle), the vestry (beside the chancel), the study (a chamber's corner), the carrel (a window-light) are DERIVED cells; the contracts size cells independently.
8. **Three new `lightReq` values and one `approach` value**: `GROUND_LEVEL_SPLAY` (the charnel's barred light-well — light and visibility from outside), `SIDE_WINDOWS_BOTH` at 5½–7½ ft centres (the library's law), `OWN_WINDOW` (the study closet's single light); `approach: NONE` (the anchorhold — a cell with no door; the validator's reachability must accept it) and `approach: HATCH_ONLY` in a faith register (the text store).
9. **GALLERY needs `functions[]` and three positions** (inside the envelope = cloister walk; round the focus = ambulatory; outside the wall = the stave svalgang) and a `walkable: bool` (the triforium degenerates into a blind arcade by era).
10. **Four corridor LICENCE mechanisms** (three attested pre-1650): program (the slype, 12th c.; the college latrine passage 1380s; the madrasa 1233), surveillance (Keble 1868 — post-dates but names the mechanism), sight-line (Maragheh's meridian corridor 1259), and fashion (the addendum's 17th-c. house) — the addendum's "the cell is early, the plan's licence is late" stands and gains two mechanisms.
11. **A PRECINCT container** (72 buildings at Rievaulx in 1538; the close of 85 acres; the külliye; the enclos) — shared need with R-INST-1's castle/palace; the one-lot-one-institution model cannot hold it.
12. **A hosted institution whose HOST DIES** (the chantry school 1547; the healer's monastery 1536–40; the west-gallery band 19th c.) — `hostedIn` needs a dated fallback; and a NO_BUILDING site that a later faith building REPLACES (the church on the grove) — `supersededBy`.
13. **`movable: true` on a BUILDING** (the stave church moved three times; the temporary anatomy theatre "dismantled annually"), and a cell whose roof is a moving fixture (the observatory crypt).
14. **Count-per-tier is an ERA prior, not a population prior** (London 108 / Norwich 58 / Winchester 57 / Canterbury 22 / York 35 → 25 / Lincoln 46 → 9) — the catalog's parish bands are fine as bounded possibilities and must not be read as "one church per N souls".
15. **The catalog's "10,000+ only" on the cathedral** is a status proxy the register does not observe (St Davids 1,751; Ribe; Wells) — keep as a default, accept a `see: bool` override.

### §Σ.3 · The `NO_BUILDING` / HOSTED verdicts (ODQ §462, tested on 22 entries)

| Entry | Verdict | Upgrade path |
|---|---|---|
| Wayside shrine (thorp, hamlet) | NO_BUILDING (a fixture on a road junction) | SHRINE_HOUSE micro-cell ≤ 10 m² at a spring/route (hamlet) |
| Access to parish church (thorp, hamlet REQ) | NO_BUILDING (a 2–5 km walk) | HOSTED oratory (manor) by household licence; OWN chapel of ease by bishop's licence with a RIGHTS vector |
| Parish church (village+) | OWN BUILDING at every tier | — |
| Village musician | NONE_HOSTED (nave on feast days; hall gallery; alehouse; west gallery 1700–1850) | never |
| Priest (resident) | OWN BUILDING — a DWELLING with a study; HOSTED porch-chamber floor | tithe barn as a 2nd lot at the RECTORY tenure |
| Graveyard | NO_BUILDING (an enclosed acre + fixtures) | lychgate LOBBY; charnel cell under the aisle (town); church house (SW England 1500–1650) |
| Druid Circle / Elder Grove Council | NO_BUILDING (enclosure + ring + avenue; the council hosted on the ground) | optional micro-cella; the buried grove → UNDERCITY |
| Healer (divine) | NONE_HOSTED, MOBILE (patient's house; priest's house; cottage; monastery infirmary) | the hospital (R-INST-4) at town |
| Monastery or friary / Multiple / Major | OWN PRECINCT | floor below town = the manorial CELL (church + house) |
| Cathedral / Great cathedral | OWN BUILDING + CLOSE | licensed by a SEE, not by 10,000 |
| Bardic college / Academy | OWN COURT (+ schools quad, theatre, observatory) | floor = HOSTED in burgage halls and the parish church (pre-1320 university) |
| Sage's quarter | OWN street of dwellings-with-study, HOSTED by a collegiate hall | — |
| Great library | OWN BUILDING at the endowment ceiling | floor = a chest; then a room over a walk (HOSTED) |
| (no row) school / library / university / anchorhold / observatory / hermitage / archive / scriptorium | HOSTED (school, library, archive, anchorhold) · OWN micro (hermitage) · OWN one-off (observatory) · NO ROW NEEDED (scriptorium → carrels + study closets) | endowment |
